+// #320 far function pointers (WDC 65816). A `far` (== `long_call`) function is
+// called through the 24-bit far-indirect sequence: clang stashes the function's
+// full 24-bit address into the runtime slot __mos_far_target and JSLs the bank-0
+// trampoline __call_indir_far (which `jml (__mos_far_target)`s across the bank);
+// the far target RTLs back. The function itself must be placed far (e.g.
+// section(".far_text")) so it returns via RTL. Shares the far/long_call spelling
+// with MipsLongCall via ParseKind="LongCall" (see handleLongCallAttr).
+def MOSFarCall : DeclOrTypeAttr, TargetSpecificAttr<TargetMOS> {
+  let Spellings = [GCC<"long_call">, GCC<"far">];
+  // No Subjects: like the calling-convention DeclOrTypeAttrs (e.g. FastCall),
+  // `far` rides a function-pointer TYPE (typedef/var) as well as a function
+  // decl, so a [Function]-only appertainment check would spuriously warn
+  // "only applies to functions" on a far function-pointer typedef. The type
+  // pass (handleFunctionTypeAttr) is what enforces it must be a function type.
+  let Documentation = [Undocumented];
+  let ParseKind = "LongCall";
+}
+

   let Subjects = SubjectList<[Function]>;

 MOSTargetInfo::MOSTargetInfo(const llvm::Triple &Triple, const TargetOptions &)
     : TargetInfo(Triple) {
+  // Must match the backend's MOSDataLayout (MOSTargetMachine.cpp). p2:32:8 =
+  // addrspace 2 "far" (32-bit ptr; low 24 bits emitted as 65816 absolute-long).
+  // p3:24:8 = addrspace 3 "packed far" (3-byte storage form of a far pointer;

@@ -204,6 +208,10 @@ uint64_t MOSTargetInfo::getPointerWidthV(LangAS AddrSpace) const {
   switch (toTargetAddressSpace(AddrSpace)) {
   case 1: // Zero page memory
     return 8;
+  case 2: // Far: a 24-bit address held in a 32-bit pointer (datalayout p2:32:8).
+    return 32; // #320 — match the IR width so sizeof(far*) == 4.
+  case 3: // Packed far: 24-bit (3-byte) storage form of a far pointer (datalayout p3:24:8).
+    return 24; // #320 — sizeof(__packed_far *) == 3.
   default:
     return 16;
   }

+// #320(a) MOS far function pointers. Return the distinct-named addrspace(2) data
+// alias of a far function symbol: `.set __mos_far_<Sym>, <Sym>` + an external AS2
+// i8 global. Referencing the function through a DISTINCT-named AS2 data symbol
+// gives a genuine far (p2) value whose bank byte survives — a same-named alias of
+// the AS0 function collapses to a bankless near address, and an addrspacecast
+// AS0->AS2 zero-extends/drops the bank. The MOS backend materializes the full
+// 24-bit address (#mos24segmentlo/hi + #mos24bank, R_MOS_ADDR24) from the p2
+// reference. Emitted exactly once per target symbol.
+// See docs/plans/2026-06-21-320-far-calls-followups.md.
+llvm::GlobalVariable *CodeGenModule::getMOSFarDataAlias(StringRef Sym) {
+  std::string FarName = (llvm::Twine("__mos_far_") + Sym).str();
+  if (llvm::GlobalVariable *GV = getModule().getNamedGlobal(FarName))
+    return GV;
+  getModule().appendModuleInlineAsm((llvm::Twine(".globl ") + FarName +
+                                     "\n.set " + FarName + ", " + Sym + "\n")
+                                        .str());
+  return new llvm::GlobalVariable(
+      getModule(), llvm::Type::getInt8Ty(getLLVMContext()), /*isConstant=*/true,
+      llvm::GlobalValue::ExternalLinkage, /*Initializer=*/nullptr, FarName,
+      /*InsertBefore=*/nullptr, llvm::GlobalValue::NotThreadLocal,
+      /*AddressSpace=*/2);
+}
+
+// Stash a 24-bit far target (an i32) into the runtime slot @__mos_far_target and
+// return a callee that JSLs the bank-0 trampoline __call_indir_far (typed with
+// the target's exact signature so MOSCallLowering forwards the arg registers
+// untouched). Shared by the direct far-call path (target = ptrtoint of the AS2
+// alias of a known far symbol) and the indirect far-call path (target = ptrtoint
+// of a loaded far function-pointer value). The volatile store of @__mos_far_target
+// must be the last thing before the call so a nested far call in an argument
+// can't clobber the single slot first.
+static CGCallee emitMOSFarStashAndThunk(CodeGenFunction &CGF,
+                                        const CGFunctionInfo &FnInfo,
+                                        llvm::Value *TargetI32) {
+  CodeGenModule &CGM = CGF.CGM;
+  llvm::GlobalVariable *Slot =
+      CGM.getModule().getOrInsertGlobal("__mos_far_target", CGF.Int32Ty);
+  CGF.Builder.CreateAlignedStore(TargetI32, Slot, CharUnits::One(),
+                                 /*IsVolatile=*/true);
+  llvm::FunctionCallee Thunk = CGM.CreateRuntimeFunction(
+      CGM.getTypes().GetFunctionType(FnInfo), "__call_indir_far");
+  return CGCallee::forDirect(Thunk);
+}
+
+// Direct call to a `far` function (the target symbol is known): materialize its
+// 24-bit address from the AS2 alias, then stash-then-thunk.
+static CGCallee emitMOSFarIndirectCallee(CodeGenFunction &CGF,
+                                         const CGFunctionInfo &FnInfo,
+                                         StringRef Sym) {
+  llvm::Constant *FarAddr = llvm::ConstantExpr::getPtrToInt(
+      CGF.CGM.getMOSFarDataAlias(Sym), CGF.Int32Ty);
+  return emitMOSFarStashAndThunk(CGF, FnInfo, FarAddr);
+}
+
 RValue CodeGenFunction::EmitCall(QualType CalleeType,
                                  const CGCallee &OrigCallee, const CallExpr *E,
                                  ReturnValueSlot ReturnValue,
@@ -7102,6 +7176,29 @@ RValue CodeGenFunction::EmitCall(QualType CalleeType,
     }
   }
 
+  // #320(a) MOS far function pointers: a call whose target carries the
+  // `far`/`long_call` attribute is dispatched through the 24-bit far-indirect
+  // trampoline (stash the target into @__mos_far_target, then call the bank-0
+  // thunk @__call_indir_far with the original args). The store must be the last
+  // thing before the call so a nested far call in an argument can't clobber the
+  // slot first; here all arguments are already evaluated (EmitCallArgs above).
+  if (const auto *FarFD = dyn_cast_or_null<FunctionDecl>(TargetDecl);
+      FarFD && FarFD->hasAttr<MOSFarCallAttr>()) {
+    // Direct call to a far function: the symbol is known.
+    Callee = emitMOSFarIndirectCallee(*this, FnInfo,
+                                      CGM.getMangledName(GlobalDecl(FarFD)));
+  } else if (const auto *FarFT = PointeeType->getAs<FunctionType>();
+             FarFT && FarFT->getExtInfo().getFar()) {
+    // Indirect call through a far function-POINTER value (the `far` attribute
+    // rides the pointee function type). The loaded callee is a ptr addrspace(2)
+    // (32-bit) holding the 24-bit target; ptrtoint it into the slot. (The
+    // 24-bit address can't ride the IR call callee, so the callee pointer is
+    // never used as such — it is stashed and the thunk JMLs through it.)
+    llvm::Value *Target =
+        Builder.CreatePtrToInt(Callee.getFunctionPointer(), Int32Ty);
+    Callee = emitMOSFarStashAndThunk(*this, FnInfo, Target);
+  }
+
   llvm::CallBase *LocalCallOrInvoke = nullptr;
   RValue Call = EmitCall(FnInfo, Callee, ReturnValue, Args, &LocalCallOrInvoke,
                          E == MustTailCall, E->getExprLoc());

+    // #320: a far function pointer (the MOS `far` attribute set the far bit on
+    // the pointee function type) holds a 24-bit address; lower it to the far
+    // (addrspace 2) data pointer so it is a 32-bit IR pointer, like a far data
+    // pointer. getTargetAddressSpace() maps a bare function pointee to the
+    // program address space (0), so override it here.

+        AS = Context.getTargetAddressSpace(getLangASFromTargetAS(2));
     ResultType = llvm::PointerType::get(getLLVMContext(), AS);
     break;
   }

+static void handleLongCallAttr(Sema &S, Decl *D, const ParsedAttr &AL) {
+  // far / long_call is owned by both MOS and MIPS (a shared GNU spelling, like
+  // `interrupt`), so it parses to a single AT_LongCall kind; dispatch on the
+  // active target. On MOS (#320) it marks a function whose calls go through the
+  // 24-bit far-indirect sequence; on MIPS it is the backend long-call hint.
+  // Subject (must-be-a-function) and target appertainment are already enforced
+  // by the auto-generated ParsedAttrInfoLongCall before we get here.
+  if (S.Context.getTargetInfo().getTriple().getArch() == llvm::Triple::mos)
+    handleSimpleAttribute<MOSFarCallAttr>(S, D, AL);
+  else
+    handleSimpleAttribute<MipsLongCallAttr>(S, D, AL);
+}

+/// Return the common width in bits of the registers assigned to one inline asm
+/// operand, or 0 if the operand has no registers or mixes register widths.
+static unsigned getAsmRegPieceSize(ArrayRef<Register> Regs,
+                                   const TargetRegisterInfo &TRI,
+                                   const MachineRegisterInfo &MRI) {
+  if (Regs.empty())
+    return 0;
+  unsigned PieceSize = TRI.getRegSizeInBits(Regs[0], MRI);
+  for (Register Reg : Regs.drop_front())
+    if (TRI.getRegSizeInBits(Reg, MRI) != PieceSize)
+      return 0;
+  return PieceSize;
+}
+
+/// Check that an operand occupying several registers can be split or merged,
+/// and report the common register width. Such a value is laid out least
+/// significant piece first, so only little-endian data layouts are supported:
+/// SelectionDAG's getCopyToParts/getCopyFromParts swap the halves for big
+/// endian, and that path has no coverage here.
+static unsigned getSplittableAsmRegPieceSize(ArrayRef<Register> Regs,
+                                             MachineIRBuilder &MIRBuilder) {
+  const MachineFunction &MF = MIRBuilder.getMF();
+  if (!MF.getDataLayout().isLittleEndian()) {
+    LLVM_DEBUG(dbgs() << "Inline asm operands spanning several registers are "
+                         "only supported on little-endian targets\n");
+    return 0;
+  }
+  unsigned PieceSize = getAsmRegPieceSize(
+      Regs, *MF.getSubtarget().getRegisterInfo(), *MIRBuilder.getMRI());
+  if (!PieceSize)
+    LLVM_DEBUG(dbgs() << "Inline asm operand mixes register widths\n");
+  return PieceSize;
+}
+
+/// Split \p Src across the several registers \p Regs assigned to one inline
+/// asm operand, least significant piece first. This mirrors what SelectionDAG's
+/// RegsForValue::getCopyToRegs emits for the same asm: the value is truncated
+/// or extended to the registers' combined width and then chopped up with a
+/// shift and a truncate per piece, as getCopyToParts does. A G_UNMERGE_VALUES
+/// would be more compact, but some targets declare a scalar-to-scalar unmerge
+/// legal without being able to select it (see the TODO in
+/// AArch64InstructionSelector::selectUnmergeValues), so the shift form is used
+/// instead; it costs nothing after legalization.
+static bool buildSplitToAsmRegs(ArrayRef<Register> Regs, Register Src,
+                                MachineIRBuilder &MIRBuilder) {
+  MachineRegisterInfo &MRI = *MIRBuilder.getMRI();
+
+  unsigned PieceSize = getSplittableAsmRegPieceSize(Regs, MIRBuilder);
+  if (!PieceSize)
+    return false;
+
+  LLT SrcTy = MRI.getType(Src);
+  if (!SrcTy.isValid() || !SrcTy.isScalar()) {
+    LLVM_DEBUG(dbgs() << "Can't split a non-scalar input across several "
+                         "inline asm registers\n");
+    return false;
+  }
+
+  unsigned WideSize = PieceSize * Regs.size();
+  LLT WideTy = LLT::integer(WideSize);
+  LLT PieceTy = LLT::integer(PieceSize);
+
+  Register Wide = Src;
+  if (SrcTy.getSizeInBits() > WideSize)
+    Wide = MIRBuilder.buildTrunc(WideTy, Src).getReg(0);
+  else if (SrcTy.getSizeInBits() < WideSize)
+    Wide = MIRBuilder.buildAnyExt(WideTy, Src).getReg(0);
+
+  for (unsigned I = 0, E = Regs.size(); I != E; ++I) {
+    Register Piece = Wide;
+    if (I) {
+      auto Amt = MIRBuilder.buildConstant(WideTy, I * PieceSize);
+      Piece = MIRBuilder.buildLShr(WideTy, Wide, Amt).getReg(0);
+    }
+    if (WideSize != PieceSize)
+      Piece = MIRBuilder.buildTrunc(PieceTy, Piece).getReg(0);
+    MIRBuilder.buildCopy(Regs[I], Piece);
+  }
+  return true;
+}
+
+/// Merge the several registers \p Regs assigned to one inline asm output into
+/// a single generic register, least significant piece first -- the inverse of
+/// buildSplitToAsmRegs, and the equivalent of SelectionDAG's
+/// RegsForValue::getCopyFromRegs. Returns an invalid register on failure.
+static Register buildMergeFromAsmRegs(ArrayRef<Register> Regs,
+                                      MachineIRBuilder &MIRBuilder) {
+  unsigned PieceSize = getSplittableAsmRegPieceSize(Regs, MIRBuilder);
+  if (!PieceSize)
+    return Register();
+
+  LLT PieceTy = LLT::integer(PieceSize);
+  SmallVector<Register, 4> Parts;
+  for (Register Reg : Regs)
+    Parts.push_back(MIRBuilder.buildCopy(PieceTy, Reg).getReg(0));
+
+  return MIRBuilder
+      .buildMergeValues(LLT::integer(PieceSize * Regs.size()), Parts)
+      .getReg(0);
+}

+  // SelectionDAG does for "=*r"-style constraints.

+    Register Val;

+      auto Copy = MIRBuilder.buildCopy(LLT::integer(SrcSize), SrcReg);
+      Val = MIRBuilder.buildTrunc(ValTy, Copy).getReg(0);
+    } else if (ValTy.getSizeInBits() == SrcSize) {
+      Val = MIRBuilder.buildCopy(ValTy, SrcReg).getReg(0);

+                           "single virtual register\n");
+      return false;
+    }

+        MachinePointerInfo(OpInfo.CallOperandVal), MachineMemOperand::MOStore,
+        ValTy, DL.getABITypeAlign(OpInfo.IndirectTy));
+    MIRBuilder.buildStore(Val, PtrRegs[0], *MMO);
+  }
+
   return true;

+  // A target's spill hook may need scratch registers and express them as extra
+  // virtual defs on the spill or reload it builds; getVDefInterval() above gives
+  // those registers live intervals, and the allocator assigns them like any
+  // other. Erasing the instruction would orphan such a register: its only def
+  // is gone, but its assignment stays in the allocator's interference matrix as
+  // a segment over a SlotIndex that no longer has an instruction. A later live
+  // range split then reads that index back as "last interference" and asserts
+  // (`enterIntvAfter called with invalid index`) or, without assertions,
+  // dereferences null. Keep the redundant access instead -- it is correct, just
+  // not free -- and let the normal spillAroundUses path rewrite it.
+  for (const MachineOperand &MO : MI->all_defs())
+    if (MO.getReg().isVirtual() && MO.getReg() != Reg)
+      return false;

+        // partial redef always kills and redefines the super-register.
+        if ((MO.readsReg() && (MO.isDef() || MO.isKill())) ||
+            (MO.isDef() && subRegLiveThrough(MI, PhysReg)))
+          SuperKills.push_back(PhysReg);
+
+        if (MO.isDef()) {
+          // Also add implicit defs for the super-register.
+          if (MO.isDead())
+            SuperDeads.push_back(PhysReg);
+          else
+            SuperDefs.push_back(PhysReg);
+        }
+      } else {
+        if (MO.isUse()) {
+          if (readsUndefSubreg(MO))
+            // We need to add an <undef> flag if the subregister is
+            // completely undefined (and we are not adding super-register
+            // defs).
+            MO.setIsUndef(true);
+        } else if (!MO.isDead()) {
+          assert(MO.isDef());
+          if (MO.isUndef()) {
+            const LiveInterval &LI = LIS->getInterval(VirtReg);
+
+            LaneBitmask LiveOutUndefLanes =
+                liveOutUndefPhiLanesForUndefSubregDef(LI, *MI.getParent(),
+                                                      SubReg, PhysReg, MI);
+            if (LiveOutUndefLanes.any()) {
+              SmallVector<unsigned, 16> CoveringIndexes;
+
+              // TODO: Just use one super register def if none of the lanes
+              // are needed?
+              if (!TRI->getCoveringSubRegIndexes(MRI->getRegClass(VirtReg),
+                                                 LiveOutUndefLanes,
+                                                 CoveringIndexes))
+                llvm_unreachable("cannot represent required subregister defs");
+
+              // Try to represent the minimum needed live out def as a
+              // sequence of subregister defs.
+              //
+              // FIXME: It would be better if we could directly represent
+              // liveness with a lanemask instead of spamming operands.
+              for (unsigned SubIdx : CoveringIndexes)
+                SuperDefs.push_back(TRI->getSubReg(PhysReg, SubIdx));
+            }
+          }
         }
-        // Rewrite. Note we could have used MachineOperand::substPhysReg(), but
-        // we need the inlining here.
-        MO.setReg(PhysReg);
-        MO.setIsRenamable(true);
       }
 
-      // Add any missing super-register kills after rewriting the whole
-      // instruction.
-      while (!SuperKills.empty())
-        MI.addRegisterKilled(SuperKills.pop_back_val(), TRI, true);
+      // The def undef and def internal flags only make sense for
+      // sub-register defs, and we are substituting a full physreg.  An
+      // implicit killed operand from the SuperKills list will represent the
+      // partial read of the super-register.
+      if (MO.isDef()) {
+        MO.setIsUndef(false);
+        MO.setIsInternalRead(false);
+      }
+
+      // PhysReg operands cannot have subregister indexes.
+      PhysReg = TRI->getSubReg(PhysReg, SubReg);
+      assert(PhysReg.isValid() && "Invalid SubReg for physical register");
+      MO.setSubReg(0);
+    }
+    // Rewrite. Note we could have used MachineOperand::substPhysReg(), but
+    // we need the inlining here.
+    MO.setReg(PhysReg);
+    MO.setIsRenamable(true);
+  }
+
+  // Add any missing super-register kills after rewriting the whole
+  // instruction.
+  while (!SuperKills.empty())
+    MI.addRegisterKilled(SuperKills.pop_back_val(), TRI, true);
 
-      while (!SuperDeads.empty())
-        MI.addRegisterDead(SuperDeads.pop_back_val(), TRI, true);
+  while (!SuperDeads.empty())
+    MI.addRegisterDead(SuperDeads.pop_back_val(), TRI, true);
 
-      while (!SuperDefs.empty())
-        MI.addRegisterDefined(SuperDefs.pop_back_val(), TRI);
+  while (!SuperDefs.empty())
+    MI.addRegisterDefined(SuperDefs.pop_back_val(), TRI);
 
-      LLVM_DEBUG(dbgs() << "> " << MI);
+  LLVM_DEBUG(dbgs() << "> " << MI);
 
-      expandCopyBundle(MI);
+  expandCopyBundle(MI);
+
+  if (!MI.isIdentityCopy())
+    return;
+  LLVM_DEBUG(dbgs() << "Identity copy: " << MI);
+  ++NumIdCopies;
+
+  Register DstReg = MI.getOperand(0).getReg();
+
+  // We may have deferred allocation of the virtual register, and the rewrite
+  // regs code doesn't handle the liveness update.
+  if (DstReg.isVirtual())
+    return;
+
+  RewriteRegs.insert(DstReg);
 
-      // We can remove identity copies right now.
-      handleIdentityCopy(MI);
+  // Copies like:
+  //    %r0 = COPY undef %r0
+  //    %al = COPY %al, implicit-def %eax
+  // give us additional liveness information: The target (super-)register
+  // must not be valid before this point. Replace the COPY with a KILL
+  // instruction to maintain this information.
+  if (CopyHasUndefLanes || MI.getOperand(1).isUndef() ||
+      MI.getNumOperands() > 2) {
+    MI.setDesc(TII->get(TargetOpcode::KILL));
+    LLVM_DEBUG(dbgs() << "  replace by: " << MI);
+    return;
+  }
+
+  if (Indexes)
+    Indexes->removeSingleMachineInstrFromMaps(MI);
+  MI.eraseFromBundle();
+  LLVM_DEBUG(dbgs() << "  deleted.\n");
+}
+
+void VirtRegRewriter::rewrite() {
+  for (MachineFunction::iterator MBBI = MF->begin(), MBBE = MF->end();
+       MBBI != MBBE; ++MBBI) {
+    LLVM_DEBUG(MBBI->print(dbgs(), Indexes));
+    for (MachineInstr &MI : llvm::make_early_inc_range(MBBI->instrs())) {
+      rewriteInstruction(MI);
     }
   }

+// A 24-bit address prints its width explicitly whenever the bare text could be
+// re-parsed as something narrower. `lda far_sym` is indistinguishable from a
+// 16-bit absolute load, so reassembling printed assembly -- which is what
+// -save-temps does -- silently selects the near instruction: a far load or
+// store loses its bank byte and becomes DBR-relative, and a long jump ($5C)
+// becomes a bank-local one ($4C). Wrapping in mos24() pins the width; the
+// assembler already honours the modifier, and $5C needs no mnemonic of its own
+// once its operand says how wide it is.
+//
+// The wrapper is omitted where the text is already unambiguous, so ordinary
+// disassembly of a bank-non-zero address stays readable.
+void MOSInstPrinter::printAddr24Operand(const MCInst *MI, unsigned OpNo,
+                                        raw_ostream &OS) {
+  const MCOperand &Op = MI->getOperand(OpNo);
+
+  // A constant that cannot fit a 16-bit operand already states its own width.
+  if (Op.isImm() && (Op.getImm() < 0 || Op.getImm() > 0xFFFF))
+    return printOperand(MI, OpNo, OS);
+
+  // An expression that already carries a MOS modifier prints that modifier
+  // itself; wrapping it again would yield mos24(mos24(x)).
+  if (Op.isExpr())
+    if (const auto *MME = dyn_cast<MOSMCExpr>(Op.getExpr()))
+      if (MME->getKind() != MOSMCExpr::VK_NONE)
+        return printOperand(MI, OpNo, OS);
+
+  OS << "mos24(";
+  printOperand(MI, OpNo, OS);
+  OS << ')';
+}
+
+// A 16-bit immediate prints its width explicitly whenever the bare text could
+// be re-parsed as an 8-bit one. The assembler has no notion of the M/X flags,
+// so `adc #66` always matches the narrowest candidate and reassembles to two
+// bytes; with M=0 the processor then consumes the following opcode as the
+// immediate's high byte and the whole instruction stream desynchronises.
+// Values above 255 are unambiguous already, which is why only small constants
+// need an explicit width.
+//
+// This is the same rule MOSDisassembler::getInstruction applies when it
+// re-wraps a decoded 16-bit immediate, one layer over: here it covers every
+// MCInst that reaches the printer, including the ones built by codegen, which
+// is the only producer of a plain (unwrapped) small 16-bit immediate.
+void MOSInstPrinter::printImm16Operand(const MCInst *MI, unsigned OpNo,
+                                       raw_ostream &OS) {
+  const MCOperand &Op = MI->getOperand(OpNo);
+
+  // An expression that already carries a MOS modifier prints that modifier
+  // itself; wrapping it again would yield mos16(mos16(x)).
+  if (Op.isExpr())
+    if (const auto *MME = dyn_cast<MOSMCExpr>(Op.getExpr()))
+      if (MME->getKind() != MOSMCExpr::VK_NONE)
+        return printOperand(MI, OpNo, OS);
+
+  // A constant that cannot fit an 8-bit immediate already states its own
+  // width, so ordinary disassembly does not grow a wrapper. Anything else --
+  // a small constant, an out-of-range one (whose bare text matches no operand
+  // at all), or a symbolic expression (which always matches the narrowest
+  // candidate) -- has to say how wide it is.
+  int64_t Value;
+  bool IsConstant = Op.isImm();
+  if (IsConstant)
+    Value = Op.getImm();
+  else if (Op.isExpr())
+    IsConstant = Op.getExpr()->evaluateAsAbsolute(Value);
+  if (IsConstant && Value > 0xFF && Value <= 0xFFFF)
+    return printOperand(MI, OpNo, OS);
+
+  OS << "mos16(";
+  printOperand(MI, OpNo, OS);
+  OS << ')';
+}
+

   const MCOperand &Op = MI->getOperand(OpNo);

+  // Symbol names that also denote registers need quotes in operand position.
+  // The parser recognizes both prefixed names and their unprefixed aliases.
+  const auto ReserveName = [&](StringRef Name) {

+  for (StringRef Name : {"sp", "r", "rp", "ya", "psw"})
+    ReserveName(Name);
+}

+// Re-tag the symbol reference inside a lowered constant expr with a relocation
+// specifier (the byte-selector for a 24-bit address). The specifier must ride on the
+// MCSymbolRefExpr itself (as the jump-table low/high-byte emission does) — wrapping in
+// a MOSMCExpr instead loses it, because MOSMCExpr::evaluateAsRelocatableImpl drops the
+// variant on a relocatable (symbol) result. Handles a bare symbol and a symbol+offset
+// (a GEP'd far array element, e.g. &table_targets[i]); returns null for any other shape
+// so the caller can fall back to the generic path.
+static const MCExpr *retagFarByte(const MCExpr *E, MOSMCExpr::VariantKind VK,
+                                  MCContext &Ctx) {
+  if (const auto *SRE = dyn_cast<MCSymbolRefExpr>(E))
+    return MCSymbolRefExpr::create(&SRE->getSymbol(), VK, Ctx);
+  if (const auto *BE = dyn_cast<MCBinaryExpr>(E)) {
+    const MCExpr *LHS = retagFarByte(BE->getLHS(), VK, Ctx);
+    if (!LHS)
+      return nullptr;
+    return MCBinaryExpr::create(BE->getOpcode(), LHS, BE->getRHS(), Ctx);
+  }
+  return nullptr;
+}
+
+// #320 packed-24: a static AS_FarPacked (addrspace 3) pointer is a 24-bit far address
+// stored in a 3-byte slot. The generic data path emits it as emitValue(ME, 3), but
+// MCFixup::getDataKindForSize(3) is unreachable (only 1/2/4/8 exist) so it degrades to a
+// single 1-byte R_MOS_ADDR8 on the low byte -> a link error (relocation out of range) and
+// a dropped bank. Emit the three address bytes explicitly with the ADDR24 segment-lo /
+// segment-hi / bank relocations (lld writes one byte each) -> the full 24-bit address,
+// bank intact -- the static-data mirror of the runtime far-address materialization
+// (selectAddr/buildFarAddrWords + MOSMCInstLower). Gated strictly on AS_FarPacked, so all
+// other constants fall through to the generic emitValue path byte-for-byte unchanged.
+bool MOSAsmPrinter::emitNonStandardSizedConstant(const Constant *CV,
+                                                 const MCExpr *ME,
+                                                 uint64_t Size) {
+  auto *PT = dyn_cast<PointerType>(CV->getType());
+  if (Size != 3 || !PT || PT->getAddressSpace() != MOS::AS_FarPacked)
+    return false;
+  MCContext &Ctx = OutContext;
+  const MOSMCExpr::VariantKind Bytes[3] = {MOSMCExpr::VK_ADDR24_SEGMENT_LO,
+                                           MOSMCExpr::VK_ADDR24_SEGMENT_HI,
+                                           MOSMCExpr::VK_ADDR24_BANK};
+  const MCExpr *Tagged[3];
+  for (int I = 0; I < 3; ++I)
+    if (!(Tagged[I] = retagFarByte(ME, Bytes[I], Ctx)))
+      return false; // unexpected expr shape -> let the generic path handle it
+  for (int I = 0; I < 3; ++I)
+    OutStreamer->emitValue(Tagged[I], /*Size=*/1);
+  return true;
+}
+

 void MOSAsmPrinter::emitInstruction(const MachineInstr *MI) {
+  // #321 Phase 2 inc 2: the FUSED 16-bit-Y indirect-long indexed access —
+  // `ldy $idx ; lda [$addr],y` (load) / `ldy $idx ; sta [$addr],y` (store) —
+  // kept as one MachineInstr through register allocation and MOSInsertREPSEP
+  // so nothing (a spill reload's `sep #$10`) can land between the 16-bit `ldy`
+  // and its use. Operands: load (dst, addr, idx), store (src, addr, idx).
+  if (MI->getOpcode() == MOS::LDIndirLongYIdx ||
+      MI->getOpcode() == MOS::STIndirLongYIdx) {
+    MCOperand Idx, Addr;
+    lowerOperand(MI->getOperand(2), Idx);
+    lowerOperand(MI->getOperand(1), Addr);
+    MCInst LdY;
+    LdY.setOpcode(MOS::LDY_ZeroPage);
+    LdY.addOperand(Idx);
+    EmitToStreamer(*OutStreamer, LdY);
+    MCInst Mem;
+    Mem.setOpcode(MI->getOpcode() == MOS::LDIndirLongYIdx
+                      ? MOS::LDA_IndirectIndexedLong
+                      : MOS::STA_IndirectIndexedLong);
+    Mem.addOperand(Addr);
+    EmitToStreamer(*OutStreamer, Mem);
+    return;
+  }

   if (MCInst OutInst; lowerPseudoInstExpansion(MI, OutInst)) {
     EmitToStreamer(*OutStreamer, OutInst);

+  // function (call arg or return value) is split across registers by a custom CC
+  // assigner. Decompose the 32-bit pointer and drive each piece to its register.
+  // Dispatch on the VA count: 2 = variant (b) {16-bit offset, 8-bit bank}; 3 =
+  // variant (c) {offset.lo, offset.hi, bank} all i8 (A:X+Y). Like ARM's f64->2x GPR
+  // split, the copies are deferred via Thunk so they land after mem-loc assignments.
+  unsigned assignCustomValue(CallLowering::ArgInfo &Arg,
+                             ArrayRef<CCValAssign> VAs,
+                             std::function<void()> *Thunk = nullptr) override {
+    assert(Arg.Regs.size() == 1 && "far pointer occupies one vreg");
+    assert((VAs.size() == 2 || VAs.size() == 3) &&
+           "far-ptr split is 2 (variant b) or 3 (variant c) locations");
+
+    // ptrtoint first: generic trunc/extract can't operate on a pointer directly.
+    auto P2Int = MIRBuilder.buildPtrToInt(LLT::scalar(32), Arg.Regs[0]);
+    auto ByteAt = [&](unsigned Shift) -> Register {
+      Register Src = P2Int.getReg(0);
+      if (Shift)
+        Src = MIRBuilder
+                  .buildLShr(LLT::scalar(32), P2Int,
+                             MIRBuilder.buildConstant(LLT::scalar(32), Shift))
+                  .getReg(0);
+      return MIRBuilder.buildTrunc(LLT::scalar(8), Src).getReg(0);
+    };
+
+    SmallVector<Register, 3> Pieces;
+    if (VAs.size() == 3)
+      Pieces = {ByteAt(0), ByteAt(8), ByteAt(16)}; // (c): A=lo, X=hi, Y=bank
+    else
+      Pieces = {MIRBuilder.buildTrunc(LLT::scalar(16), P2Int).getReg(0),
+                ByteAt(16)}; // (b): RS#=offset(i16), RC#=bank(i8)
+
+    SmallVector<CCValAssign, 3> VACopies(VAs.begin(), VAs.end());
+    auto DoAssign = [this, Pieces, VACopies]() {
+      for (unsigned I = 0; I < Pieces.size(); ++I)
+        assignValueToReg(Pieces[I], VACopies[I].getLocReg(), VACopies[I]);
+    };
+    if (Thunk)
+      *Thunk = DoAssign;
+    else
+      DoAssign();
+    return VAs.size();
+  }

 #include "MOSCallingConv.h"
 
+#include "MOSSubtarget.h"
 #include "llvm/CodeGen/MachineFunction.h"
 #include "llvm/IR/DataLayout.h"
 
 #include "MOSGenCallingConv.inc"
+
+// #320 Inc 4 Ph2: far-pointer CC variant selectors (declared in MOSCallingConv.h,
+// referenced by the gated far-ptr rules in MOSCallingConv.td). Read the
+// off-by-default +mos-farcc-* feature from the current function's subtarget. With
+// none set, farPtrCC() == None and all return false, so the far-ptr rules never
+// match and default codegen is byte-identical.
+bool llvm::MOSFarCCImag32(CCState &State) {
+  return State.getMachineFunction().getSubtarget<MOSSubtarget>().farPtrCC() ==
+         MOSSubtarget::FarPtrCC::Imag32;
+}
+bool llvm::MOSFarCCSplit(CCState &State) {
+  return State.getMachineFunction().getSubtarget<MOSSubtarget>().farPtrCC() ==
+         MOSSubtarget::FarPtrCC::Split;
+}
+bool llvm::MOSFarCCAXY(CCState &State) {
+  return State.getMachineFunction().getSubtarget<MOSSubtarget>().farPtrCC() ==
+         MOSSubtarget::FarPtrCC::AXY;
+}
+bool llvm::MOSFarCCStack(CCState &State) {
+  return State.getMachineFunction().getSubtarget<MOSSubtarget>().farPtrCC() ==
+         MOSSubtarget::FarPtrCC::Stack;
+}
+
+// #320 Inc 4 Ph2 variant (b): split a 32-bit far (addrspace 2) pointer into a
+// 16-bit offset in an RS# (Imag16) pair + an 8-bit bank in an RC# (Imag8). Both
+// allocations run inside the table CC, so AllocateReg's __rc unit aliasing keeps
+// the far pointer's bytes from double-booking any near-pointer / scalar arg.
+// Pushing TWO custom CCValAssigns for the one value defers the actual
+// pointer<->{offset,bank} (de)composition to MOSCallLowering::assignCustomValue.
+// RS1..RS7 / RC2..RC9 are the caller-saved imaginary registers (the same pools the
+// near-pointer and i8 argument rules draw from).
+bool llvm::CC_MOS_FarPtrSplit(unsigned ValNo, MVT ValVT, MVT LocVT,
+                              CCValAssign::LocInfo LocInfo,
+                              ISD::ArgFlagsTy ArgFlags, CCState &State) {
+  static const MCPhysReg OffsetRegs[] = {MOS::RS1, MOS::RS2, MOS::RS3, MOS::RS4,
+                                         MOS::RS5, MOS::RS6, MOS::RS7};
+  static const MCPhysReg BankRegs[] = {MOS::RC2, MOS::RC3, MOS::RC4, MOS::RC5,
+                                       MOS::RC6, MOS::RC7, MOS::RC8, MOS::RC9};
+  MCRegister OffReg = State.AllocateReg(OffsetRegs);
+  MCRegister BankReg = State.AllocateReg(BankRegs);
+  // Out of imaginary registers: report unhandled. (MVP has no stack fallback for a
+  // far pointer; in practice 7 RS pairs is ample for realistic far-ptr arg counts.)
+  if (!OffReg || !BankReg)
+    return false;
+  State.addLoc(CCValAssign::getCustomReg(ValNo, MVT::i16, OffReg, MVT::i16,
+                                         CCValAssign::Full));
+  State.addLoc(CCValAssign::getCustomReg(ValNo, MVT::i8, BankReg, MVT::i8,
+                                         CCValAssign::Full));
+  return true;
+}
+
+// #320 Inc 4 Ph2 variant (c): carry a 32-bit far pointer in the hardware registers
+// A:X (16-bit offset: A=low, X=high) + Y (bank byte). There is no 16-bit A:X
+// register class, so the offset is TWO i8 locations — three custom CCValAssigns
+// total {A, X, Y}, (de)composed by MOSCallLowering::assignCustomValue (which
+// dispatches on the VA count: 2 -> variant b, 3 -> variant c). A/X/Y are
+// caller-saved GPRs; AllocateReg coordinates them with the i8 / near-pointer rules
+// via register-unit aliasing. A:X+Y is singular, so only ONE far pointer fits and
+// it must precede any A/X-consuming scalar arg (near-pointer args use RS#, so they
+// don't conflict); when A/X/Y are unavailable this returns false and the far ptr
+// falls through to the generic rule (which then errors) — the expected "(c) doesn't
+// scale past one" limitation.
+bool llvm::CC_MOS_FarPtrAXY(unsigned ValNo, MVT ValVT, MVT LocVT,
+                            CCValAssign::LocInfo LocInfo, ISD::ArgFlagsTy ArgFlags,
+                            CCState &State) {
+  MCRegister Lo = State.AllocateReg(MOS::A);
+  MCRegister Hi = State.AllocateReg(MOS::X);
+  MCRegister Bank = State.AllocateReg(MOS::Y);
+  if (!Lo || !Hi || !Bank)
+    return false;
+  State.addLoc(
+      CCValAssign::getCustomReg(ValNo, MVT::i8, Lo, MVT::i8, CCValAssign::Full));
+  State.addLoc(
+      CCValAssign::getCustomReg(ValNo, MVT::i8, Hi, MVT::i8, CCValAssign::Full));
+  State.addLoc(CCValAssign::getCustomReg(ValNo, MVT::i8, Bank, MVT::i8,
+                                         CCValAssign::Full));
+  return true;
+}

+  // #320 Inc 4 Ph2: a 32-bit far (addrspace 2) pointer crossing a call. Each

+// #321 Increment 1b: returns the G_GLOBAL_VALUE def of `Ptr` iff `Ptr` is a
+// plain near (addrspace 0) global address — the addressing mode the 16-bit
+// LDA/ADC/STA absolute forms can take directly. Null otherwise.
+static MachineInstr *nearAbsGlobalDef(Register Ptr,
+                                      const MachineRegisterInfo &MRI) {
+  using namespace TargetOpcode;
+  if (!Ptr.isVirtual() || MRI.getType(Ptr).getAddressSpace() != 0)
+    return nullptr;
+  MachineInstr *Def = MRI.getVRegDef(Ptr);
+  if (!Def || Def->getOpcode() != G_GLOBAL_VALUE)
+    return nullptr;
+  return Def;
+}
+
+// #321 Increment 1b: a single-use 16-bit plain G_LOAD from a near abs global, or
+// null. (The kind of operand the 16-bit lda/ALU-abs forms can take directly.)
+static GLoad *nearAbsLoad(Register R, const MachineRegisterInfo &MRI) {
+  if (MRI.getType(R) != LLT::scalar(16) || !MRI.hasOneNonDBGUse(R))
+    return nullptr;
+  auto *Ld = dyn_cast_or_null<GLoad>(MRI.getVRegDef(R));
+  if (!Ld || Ld->getOpcode() != TargetOpcode::G_LOAD ||
+      !nearAbsGlobalDef(Ld->getPointerReg(), MRI))
+    return nullptr;
+  return Ld;
+}
+
+// #321 Increment 1b: is this a commutative 16-bit ALU op (operands may be
+// swapped freely)? Subtract is excluded — order-sensitive.
+static bool isCommutativeAlu(unsigned Opc) {
+  using namespace TargetOpcode;
+  return Opc == G_ADD || Opc == G_AND || Opc == G_OR || Opc == G_XOR;
+}

@@ -285,6 +285,38 @@ void MOSFrameLowering::emitPrologue(MachineFunction &MF,
   const TargetRegisterInfo &TRI = *MF.getRegInfo().getTargetRegisterInfo();
   MachineIRBuilder Builder(MBB, MBB.begin());
 
+  // A native-mode 65816 interrupt inherits M/X from the interrupted
+  // instruction; unlike a normal C call, entry is not guaranteed M8/X8.
+  // Save the complete physical registers at deterministic 16-bit widths, then
+  // establish the ordinary C ABI state. RTI will restore the hardware-stacked
+  // P (and therefore the interrupted M/X state) after the matching epilogue.
+  //
+  // Interrupt entry also inherits D and DBR unchanged, and user code (inline
+  // asm) may legally have moved either. Compiled code is D-relative on every
+  // imaginary-register access (including the soft-stack pointer) and
+  // DBR-relative on every absolute access, so both are saved and the C ABI
+  // state (D=0, DBR=0) re-established before any of that runs. All six added
+  // instructions are M/X-width-independent. `pea 0; pld` zeroes D without
+  // touching a register; `phk; plb` derives DBR=0 from PBR, which the
+  // interrupt entry hardware forces to 0.
+  if (isISR(MF) && MF.getSubtarget<MOSSubtarget>().hasW65816()) {
+    const TargetInstrInfo &TII = *MF.getSubtarget().getInstrInfo();
+    auto Entry = MBB.begin();
+    BuildMI(MBB, Entry, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::REP_Immediate))
+        .addImm(0x30);
+    BuildMI(MBB, Entry, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PHA_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Entry, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PHX_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Entry, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PHY_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Entry, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PHB_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Entry, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PHD_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Entry, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PEA_Absolute)).addImm(0);
+    BuildMI(MBB, Entry, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PLD_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Entry, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PHK_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Entry, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PLB_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Entry, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::SEP_Immediate))
+        .addImm(0x30);
+  }
+
   // Stack pointer adjustments need to occur after the CLD in an interrupt
   // handler or the sum might be incorrect.
   for (MachineInstr &MI : MBB)
@@ -346,6 +378,18 @@ void MOSFrameLowering::emitEpilogue(MachineFunction &MF,
   // If soft stack is used, increase the soft stack pointer SP.
   if (StackSize)
     offsetSP(Builder, StackSize);
+
+  if (isISR(MF) && MF.getSubtarget<MOSSubtarget>().hasW65816()) {
+    const TargetInstrInfo &TII = *MF.getSubtarget().getInstrInfo();
+    auto Ret = MBB.getFirstTerminator();
+    BuildMI(MBB, Ret, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::REP_Immediate)).addImm(0x30);
+    // Reverse of the prologue's push order: D and DBR were pushed last.
+    BuildMI(MBB, Ret, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PLD_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Ret, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PLB_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Ret, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PLY_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Ret, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PLX_Implied));
+    BuildMI(MBB, Ret, DebugLoc(), TII.get(MOS::PLA_Implied));

+MVT MOSTargetLowering::getPointerTy(const DataLayout &DL, uint32_t AS) const {
+  // #320 packed-24: AS_FarPacked (3) is a 24-bit pointer in MEMORY (datalayout
+  // p3:24:8) but there is NO MVT::i24, and a packed pointer is only ever held in
+  // registers as its 32-bit "far" form (it converts to/from AS_Far at load/store).
+  // The generic getPointerTy default (MVT::getIntegerVT(24)) yields an invalid MVT
+  // that crashes IR-level passes (e.g. CodeGenPrepare::optimizeLoadExt). Report the
+  // 32-bit far value type instead; the 3-byte memory footprint comes from the
+  // datalayout, not this hook, and GISel derives the 24-bit LLT from the datalayout.
+  if (AS == MOS::AS_FarPacked)
+    return MVT::i32;
+  return TargetLowering::getPointerTy(DL, AS);
+}

+// True when VT is a concrete, sized type wider than Bits. Types with no
+// meaningful width of their own (MVT::Other for an operand with no value type,
+// the abstract iPTR) are never wider than anything.
+static bool isWiderThan(MVT VT, unsigned Bits) {
+  if (!VT.isValid() || VT == MVT::iPTR ||
+      !(VT.isInteger() || VT.isFloatingPoint()))
+    return false;
+  return VT.getSizeInBits().getKnownMinValue() > Bits;
+}

   if (Constraint.size() == 1) {

       return std::make_pair(0U, &MOS::Imag8RegClass);
+    // The data registers A, X and Y are 8 bits wide and are not pairable: there
+    // is no ordering in which any two of them form a wider value, so a
+    // constraint naming one of them cannot accept an operand that does not fit
+    // in it. Reject such an operand by falling through to the generic
+    // implementation, which reports the constraint as unsatisfiable; accepting
+    // it would drop the operand's high bits. Clang states the same rule for C
+    // sources in MOSTargetInfo::validateOperandSize().
     case 'R':
+      if (isWiderThan(VT, 8))
+        break;
       return std::make_pair(0U, &MOS::GPRRegClass);
     case 'a':
+      if (isWiderThan(VT, 8))
+        break;
       return std::make_pair(MOS::A, &MOS::GPRRegClass);
     case 'x':
+      if (isWiderThan(VT, 8))
+        break;

+      if (isWiderThan(VT, 8))
+        break;

     case 'd':
+      if (isWiderThan(VT, 8))
+        break;

+  // Clang emits these for register variables even when the source constraint

+  if (Result.first && Result.second && Result.first != MOS::C &&
+      Result.first != MOS::V &&

+// #321 native s16 equality: expand a fused 16-bit compare-branch to
+// `LDA{Imag16,bs16} $l; CMP{Imag16,Abs16,Imm16} $r; BR`. The LDA/CMP are MLow=1
+// logical pseudos, so the late REP/SEP pass brackets them with rep/sep; the cmp
+// sets Z (modeled as an implicit def), and the inserted `sep` doesn't touch Z, so
+// the BR reads it correctly. Operands: (0)=tgt (1)=Flag=Z (2)=flag_val (3)=$l
+// (4)=$r. The LHS ($l) is Imag16 (CmpBrImag16/Imm16) or a near-abs GLOBAL read by
+// `lda abs` (CmpBrAbsAbs16, the v3 fold); the RHS ($r) is Imag16, an abs global
+// (`cmp abs`), or a 16-bit immediate. The two folded loads' memrefs ride on the
+// pseudo and are cloned onto the abs lda/cmp.
+void MOSInstrInfo::expandCmpBr16(MachineIRBuilder &Builder) const {
+  MachineInstr &MI = *Builder.getInsertPt();
+  const Register Flag = MI.getOperand(1).getReg();
+  const unsigned Op = MI.getOpcode();
+
+  const bool LhsAbs = Op == MOS::CmpBrAbsAbs16 || Op == MOS::CmpBrAbsImm16;
+  unsigned CmpOpc;
+  switch (Op) {
+  case MOS::CmpBrImm16:
+  case MOS::CmpBrAbsImm16:
+    CmpOpc = MOS::CMPImm16;
+    break;
+  case MOS::CmpBrAbsAbs16:
+    CmpOpc = MOS::CMPAbs16;
+    break;
+  case MOS::CmpBrImagAbs16: // LHS is Imag16 (LhsAbs=false -> LDAImag16); RHS is abs global
+    CmpOpc = MOS::CMPAbs16;
+    break;
+  default: // CmpBrImag16
+    CmpOpc = MOS::CMPImag16;
+    break;
+  }
+
+  // Post-RA: use the physical 16-bit accumulator A16 (the pseudo declares it in
+  // Defs so regalloc kept nothing live across it). lda $l (16-bit) -> A16.
+  auto LDA = Builder.buildInstr(LhsAbs ? MOS::LDAbs16 : MOS::LDAImag16)
+                 .addDef(MOS::A16)
+                 .add(MI.getOperand(3));
+  if (LhsAbs)
+    LDA.cloneMemRefs(MI);
+
+  // cmp $r (16-bit): defs C (dead) and Z (the live flag the branch reads).
+  auto CMP = Builder.buildInstr(CmpOpc)
+                 .addDef(MOS::C, RegState::Dead)
+                 .addUse(MOS::A16)
+                 .add(MI.getOperand(4));
+  if (CmpOpc == MOS::CMPAbs16)
+    CMP.cloneMemRefs(MI);
+  CMP.addDef(Flag, RegState::Implicit);
+
+  Builder.buildInstr(MOS::BR)
+      .add(MI.getOperand(0))
+      .addUse(Flag, RegState::Kill)
+      .add(MI.getOperand(2));
+
+  MI.eraseFromParent();
+}

+//===---------------------------------------------------------------------===//
+// #321 Increment 1b: 16-bit-accumulator load/add/store (native M=0 mode)
+//===---------------------------------------------------------------------===//
+//
+// These three logical ops carry a 16-bit value through the A16 accumulator
+// (class Ac16, which holds only A16 = B:A). The MC opcodes are width-agnostic —
+// the same LDA/ADC/STA absolute bytes the M flag governs at runtime — so they
+// PseudoInstExpand to the plain _Absolute forms. MLow=1 tags them so the
+// MOSInsertREPSEP pass wraps the run in one `rep #$20 … sep #$20`. Gated on
+// HasAccum16 (+mos-a16); only ever produced by selectAlu16Native, so the
+// default 8-bit codegen never sees them.
+
+// LDA abs (16-bit) — load a 16-bit absolute global into A16.
+def LDAbs16 : MOSLoad, PseudoInstExpansion<(LDA_Absolute addr16:$src)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Ac16:$dst);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins addr16:$src);
+}
+
+// ADC abs (16-bit) — A16 = A16 + [abs] + carry. Mirrors the 8-bit MOSAddSub
+// shape (carry in/out + overflow), but on Ac16 and reading absolute memory.
+def ADCAbs16 : MOSLogicalInstr, PseudoInstExpansion<(ADC_Absolute addr16:$r)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  let mayLoad = true;
+  let isAdd = true;
+  let Constraints = "$dst = $l";
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Ac16:$dst, Cc:$carryout, Vc:$vout);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$l, addr16:$r, Cc:$carryin);
+}
+
+// SBC abs (16-bit) — A16 = A16 - [abs] - !carry. Mirrors ADCAbs16; carry-in is
+// SET (sec) for a plain subtract. Non-commutative: $l is the minuend.
+def SBCAbs16 : MOSLogicalInstr, PseudoInstExpansion<(SBC_Absolute addr16:$r)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  let mayLoad = true;
+  let Constraints = "$dst = $l";
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Ac16:$dst, Cc:$carryout, Vc:$vout);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$l, addr16:$r, Cc:$carryin);
+}

+// Bitwise abs (16-bit): A16 = A16 {& | ^} [abs]. No carry/overflow — simpler than
+// the ADC/SBC shape. MLow=1 so the run is REP/SEP-bracketed.
+class MOSBitAbs16<Instruction real> : MOSLogicalInstr,

+  let Constraints = "$dst = $l";
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Ac16:$dst);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$l, addr16:$r);

+def ANDAbs16 : MOSBitAbs16<AND_Absolute>;
+def ORAAbs16 : MOSBitAbs16<ORA_Absolute>;
+def EORAbs16 : MOSBitAbs16<EOR_Absolute>;

+// #321 Increment 1d-retry: the zero-page (Imag16) analogs of the *Abs16 ops.
+// Where *Abs16 read/write an absolute global, these read/write a 16-bit value
+// resident in an Imag16 zero-page pair — so the GISel pipeline can carry an s16
+// *value* (locals, multi-use intermediates) in Imag16 and the selector emits a
+// self-contained `lda zp; (clc;) adc zp; sta zp` sequence on the transient A16.
+// The Imag16 operand lowers to its LOW-byte __rcN symbol (MOSRegisterInfo.cpp
+// sublo rule); the 16-bit width comes from the surrounding rep #$20 (MLow=1).
+// CRITICAL invariant: the accumulator is entered/left ONLY via these load/store
+// instructions — never a COPY between Ac16 and an 8-bit reg — so the register
+// coalescer can never merge an 8-bit value into A16 (the crash that sank the
+// first 1d prototype). All gated on HasAccum16, inert in default codegen.
+
+// LDA zp (16-bit) — load a 16-bit Imag16 pair into A16.
+def LDAImag16 : MOSLoad, PseudoInstExpansion<(LDA_ZeroPage addr8:$src)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Ac16:$dst);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Imag16:$src);
+}
+
+// ADC zp (16-bit) — A16 = A16 + [Imag16] + carry. Mirrors ADCAbs16.
+def ADCImag16 : MOSLogicalInstr, PseudoInstExpansion<(ADC_ZeroPage addr8:$r)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  let mayLoad = true;
+  let isAdd = true;
+  let Constraints = "$dst = $l";
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Ac16:$dst, Cc:$carryout, Vc:$vout);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$l, Imag16:$r, Cc:$carryin);
+}
+
+// SBC zp (16-bit) — A16 = A16 - [Imag16] - !carry. Mirrors SBCAbs16.
+def SBCImag16 : MOSLogicalInstr, PseudoInstExpansion<(SBC_ZeroPage addr8:$r)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  let mayLoad = true;
+  let Constraints = "$dst = $l";
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Ac16:$dst, Cc:$carryout, Vc:$vout);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$l, Imag16:$r, Cc:$carryin);
+}
+
+// Bitwise zp (16-bit): A16 = A16 {& | ^} [Imag16]. Mirrors MOSBitAbs16.
+class MOSBitImag16<Instruction real> : MOSLogicalInstr,
+    PseudoInstExpansion<(real addr8:$r)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  let mayLoad = true;
+  let Constraints = "$dst = $l";
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Ac16:$dst);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$l, Imag16:$r);
+}
+def ANDImag16 : MOSBitImag16<AND_ZeroPage>;
+def ORAImag16 : MOSBitImag16<ORA_ZeroPage>;
+def EORImag16 : MOSBitImag16<EOR_ZeroPage>;
+
+// STA zp (16-bit) — store A16 into a 16-bit Imag16 pair. Like STImag8, writing a
+// zero-page "register" DEFS it (the Imag16 is an OUT operand), so the s16 add
+// result is a real vreg the G_UNMERGE consumers read. Crucially the value enters
+// the accumulator only via LDAImag16/ADCImag16 and leaves only via this store —
+// never a COPY between Ac16 and an 8-bit reg (the 1d-prototype coalescer crash).
+def STAImag16 : MOSLogicalInstr, PseudoInstExpansion<(STA_ZeroPage addr8:$dst)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Imag16:$dst);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$src);
+}
+
+// #321 native s16 indirect memory: LDA (zp) / STA (zp) in 16-bit-accumulator mode —
+// load/store a 16-bit value through a runtime pointer held in a zero-page Imag16
+// pair. Mirror the 8-bit LDIndir/STIndir (LDA_Indirect=$B2 / STA_Indirect=$92,
+// 65C02+; the w65816 has them) with MLow=1 so MOSInsertREPSEP brackets the access.
+// The value enters/leaves A16 only via these / the Imag16 load-store — no Ac16<->
+// 8-bit COPY. Used by selectMem16Indir for *p, a[i], a[i]=v, p[i].
+def LDAIndir16 : MOSLoad, PseudoInstExpansion<(LDA_Indirect addr8:$addr)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Ac16:$dst);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Imag16:$addr);
+}
+def STAIndir16 : MOSStore, PseudoInstExpansion<(STA_Indirect addr8:$addr)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$src, Imag16:$addr);
+}

+// #321 native 16-bit compares: A16 - operand, discard the result, keep the flags
+// (Cc = carry; N/Z set implicitly). MLow=1 so MOSInsertREPSEP brackets `lda; cmp`
+// in one rep/sep run; SEP doesn't touch C/Z/N, so a following branch reads them.
+// Used by selectSbc16 for the unsigned-ordering (C-flag) s16 comparisons. There is
+// no CMP_Immediate16 opcode, so a constant RHS is materialized into Imag16 and
+// compared via CMPImag16 (correct, just not folded).
+class MOSCMP16 : MOSCMP {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  let mayLoad = true;
+}
+def CMPImag16 : MOSCMP16, PseudoInstExpansion<(CMP_ZeroPage addr8:$r)> {
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$l, Imag16:$r);
+}
+def CMPAbs16 : MOSCMP16, PseudoInstExpansion<(CMP_Absolute addr16:$r)> {
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$l, addr16:$r);
+}
+// cmp (zp) — compare A16 against a 16-bit value read through a zero-page POINTER
+// pair (M=0). The RHS-indexed compare fold: `arr[i]`/`p[i]` legalize to a computed
+// pointer + G_LOAD16_INDIR (lda (zp)); when that load is a single-use compare RHS,
+// selectSbc16 folds it here (cmp (zp)) instead of staging the value through an
+// Imag16 temp. Mirrors LDAIndir16 + the 8-bit CMPIndir; no index reg (so no X-flag
+// bracketing) and no abs base (so no frame-index / ZP-confusable concern).
+def CMPIndir16 : MOSCMP16, PseudoInstExpansion<(CMP_Indirect addr8:$addr)> {
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$l, Imag16:$addr);
+}
+// cmp #imm16 — CMP_Immediate16 is generated by the CC1_All multiclass (like
+// ADC_Immediate16), so a constant RHS folds to `cmp #$1234` (common loop bounds)
+// instead of materializing the constant into an Imag16 pair. No mayLoad.
+def CMPImm16 : MOSCMP, PseudoInstExpansion<(CMP_Immediate16 imm16:$r)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$l, imm16:$r);
+}
+
+// #321 native 16-bit constant shifts: in 16-bit-accumulator mode a single `asl a`
+// / `lsr a` shifts all 16 bits at once, so `x << k` / `x >> k` (k a compile-time
+// constant) selects to one `lda; (asl|lsr)*k; sta` run instead of the 8-bit
+// asl/rol byte-pair chain. MLow=1 so MOSInsertREPSEP brackets the run; the value
+// enters A16 only via LDAImag16 and leaves only via STAImag16 — never a COPY
+// between Ac16 and an 8-bit reg (the 1d-prototype coalescer crash). No carry
+// operand in v1: each shift self-fills 0 and consecutive shifts don't thread the
+// carry. Used by selectShift16Native. ASL covers `<<`; LSR covers unsigned `>>`;
+// signed `>>` (ASHR, needs ror+sign), rotates, and the memory forms are follow-ups.
+class MOSShiftAcc16<Instruction real> : MOSLogicalInstr,
+    PseudoInstExpansion<(real)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  let Constraints = "$dst = $src";
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Ac16:$dst);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$src);
+}
+// asl a / lsr a CLOBBER the carry (shifted-out bit), even though selectShift16Native
+// leaves the carry-out dead. Model that with Defs=[C] so the scheduler/regalloc never
+// places one inside a live-carry interval — e.g. between a `cmp` and the `bcs` that
+// reads its carry (a CRC `if (crc & 0x8000) ... crc <<= 1` hoists the common shift
+// across the test; without this the asl corrupts the branch carry -> miscompile, found
+// by the Tier-1 differential fuzzer). RORAcc16 models its carry as an operand already;
+// INCAcc16/DECAcc16 correctly do NOT clobber C (inc/dec a set only N/Z), so they stay.
+def ASLAcc16 : MOSShiftAcc16<ASL_Accumulator> { let Defs = [C]; }
+def LSRAcc16 : MOSShiftAcc16<LSR_Accumulator> { let Defs = [C]; }
+
+// #321 native s16 inc/dec: in 16-bit-accumulator mode one `inc a` / `dec a` steps all
+// 16 bits, so a 16-bit `x + 1` / `x - 1` selects to a single accumulator inc/dec
+// (1 byte, no carry-init) instead of `(clc;) adc #$0001` — and far better than the
+// 8-bit byte inc/dec-with-carry chain the default path emits. Same accumulator-RMW
+// shape as ASLAcc16 (MLow=1, $dst=$src, no extra operands); inc a / dec a set N/Z but
+// not C/V, which is correct for a plain ±1 (G_ADD/G_SUB carry out is unused here). The
+// value enters A16 only via LDAImag16/LDAbs16 and leaves only via STAImag16 (no Ac16<->
+// 8-bit COPY). Used by selectAlu16Native (register ±1 and global ±1 via lda/inc-a/sta).
+def INCAcc16 : MOSShiftAcc16<INC_Accumulator>;
+def DECAcc16 : MOSShiftAcc16<DEC_Accumulator>;
+// NB: a memory-RMW `inc abs`/`dec abs` for `g = g ± 1` is deliberately NOT provided.
+// The 65816 has no `inc long`, and `inc abs` is DBR-relative — but this platform
+// addresses all data via 24-bit long (DBR-independent) loads/stores, so a DBR-relative
+// RMW would couple correctness to DBR==0 + bank-0 LoRAM placement (it only happens to
+// work today via the low-8KB WRAM mirror). selectAlu16Native uses `lda long; inc/dec a;
+// sta long` instead — the same long addressing, just dropping the clc + #imm.
+
+// #321 native s16 arithmetic right shift (signed `>>`): the 65816 has no native
+// ASR, but in 16-bit mode `cmp #$8000` sets carry = the sign bit (A >= 0x8000) and
+// `ror a` rotates that carry back into bit 15 while shifting right — so the sign
+// replicates. selectShift16Native emits `cmp #$8000; ror a` per bit. Unlike ASL/LSR
+// this rotate has a real carry-in (the sign from the cmp); its carry-out (old bit 0)
+// is dead (the next bit's cmp recomputes C). MLow=1 so the whole run stays bracketed.
+def RORAcc16 : MOSLogicalInstr, PseudoInstExpansion<(ROR_Accumulator)> {
+  let Predicates = [HasAccum16];
+  let MLow = 1;
+  let Constraints = "$dst = $src";
+  dag OutOperandList = (outs Ac16:$dst, Cc:$carryout);
+  dag InOperandList = (ins Ac16:$src, Cc:$carryin);
+}

   switch (MI.getOpcode()) {
   case MOS::G_ADD:
   case MOS::G_SUB:
+    // #321 Increment 1d-retry: a native s16 add/sub (left un-narrowed by the
+    // legalizer under +mos-a16) carries its value in an Imag16 pair.
+    if (STI.hasAccum16() &&

+    // #321 Increment 1d-retry: native s16 bitwise lives in an Imag16 pair too.
+    if (STI.hasAccum16() &&

     // Don't inhibit generation of INC/DEC.
     if (RHSConst->Value.abs().isOne())
       return false;

+    // #321 a16 register-pressure relief: under +mos-a16, lower a SMALL-constant 8-bit
+    // add/sub to a short chain of relocatable INC/DEC (G_INC/G_DEC -> IncMB/DecMB on the
+    // Anyi8 class = A/X/Y/zp) instead of the A-pinned `add Ac,imm -> ADCImm` selImpl
+    // pattern (ADCImm constrains its operand to Ac={A} because `adc` is hardware-A-only).
+    // WHY: an A-pinned i8 loop counter — e.g. a u16-array byte index stepped `i += 2` —
+    // held live across a 16-bit indexed-load transit (Ac16 = A:B) deadlocks greedy
+    // register allocation: the counter cannot be recolored off the singleton A and the
+    // single-instruction INF Ac16 transit cannot spill (a16regpress.c: "ran out of
+    // registers"; pinpointed on an asserts build — last-chance recoloring of the {A}
+    // counter fails, so the Ac16 transit never gets $a16). A relocatable Anyi8 counter
+    // instead coalesces into the X array index (which is itself a COPY of the counter),
+    // freeing A16. selectIncDecMB constrains the chain to Anyi8 (the real relocate-off-A
+    // mechanism). Gated on hasAccum16() so the DEFAULT 8-bit build is byte-identical (it
+    // keeps the ADCImm pattern). Bounded by kMaxA16IncDecChain so the chain is never
+    // larger than clc;adc (an inc is 1 B in a register / a 65C02 `inc a`; a value that
+    // must round-trip through A for `adc` costs at least as much in every residency). A
+    // miss only keeps the ADC — never a wrong value: G_ADD/G_SUB are plain modular ops
+    // with no carry-out operand, so an inc/dec chain is value-identical (exactly as the
+    // pre-existing ±1 rewrite already relies on).
+    constexpr int64_t kMaxA16IncDecChain = 2;
+    if (STI.hasAccum16() && MRI.getType(Dst) == LLT::scalar(8)) {
+      int64_t Amt = RHSConst->Value.getSExtValue();
+      if (MI.getOpcode() == MOS::G_SUB)
+        Amt = -Amt;
+      int64_t Steps = std::abs(Amt);
+      if (Steps >= 2 && Steps <= kMaxA16IncDecChain) {
+        unsigned GOpc = Amt > 0 ? MOS::G_INC : MOS::G_DEC;
+        Register Cur = MI.getOperand(1).getReg();
+        for (int64_t I = 0; I < Steps; ++I) {
+          Register Out = (I + 1 == Steps)
+                             ? Dst
+                             : MRI.createGenericVirtualRegister(LLT::scalar(8));
+          MachineInstr *G = Builder.buildInstr(GOpc, {Out}, {Cur});
+          if (!selectIncDecMB(*G))
+            return false;
+          Cur = Out;
+        }
+        MI.eraseFromParent();
+        return true;
+      }
+    }
   }

+// #321 native s16: recover a 16-bit constant whether it survived as a G_CONSTANT or was
+// byte-split into G_MERGE_VALUES(i8 lo, i8 hi) during legalization — how a 16-bit
+// constant operand of a native EQ/ALU op reaches selection under +mos-a16 (a
+// G_CONSTANT i16 is illegal and is narrowed to bytes). Two non-constant byte loads
+// merged together yield nullopt, so a `g == h` (both variables) is not mistaken for an
+// immediate compare. Shared by the EQ matcher (CmpNZImm16_match) and getImm16Operand.
+static std::optional<int64_t> getI16Const(Register R,
+                                          const MachineRegisterInfo &MRI) {
+  if (auto C = getIConstantVRegValWithLookThrough(R, MRI))
+    return C->Value.getZExtValue() & 0xFFFF;
+  MachineInstr *Def = getDefIgnoringCopies(R, MRI);
+  if (Def && Def->getOpcode() == TargetOpcode::G_MERGE_VALUES &&
+      Def->getNumOperands() == 3) {
+    auto Lo = getIConstantVRegValWithLookThrough(Def->getOperand(1).getReg(), MRI);
+    auto Hi = getIConstantVRegValWithLookThrough(Def->getOperand(2).getReg(), MRI);
+    if (Lo && Hi)
+      return ((Hi->Value.getZExtValue() & 0xFF) << 8) |
+             (Lo->Value.getZExtValue() & 0xFF);
+  }
+  return std::nullopt;
+}

+// #321 native s16 equality: match a 16-bit G_SBC whose live flag is Z (the ==/!=
+// form left un-narrowed by legalizeICmp when the result feeds a branch). LHS/RHS are
+// the s16 operands (5/6); selectBrCondImm emits a CmpBrImag16/Imm16 fused branch.
+struct CmpNZ16_match {
+  Register &LHS;
+  Register &Flag;
+  MachineInstr *CondMI = nullptr;
+
+  CmpNZ16_match(Register &LHS, Register &Flag) : LHS(LHS), Flag(Flag) {}
+
+  bool match(const MachineRegisterInfo &MRI, Register CondReg) {
+    auto DefSrcReg = getDefSrcRegIgnoringCopies(CondReg, MRI);
+    CondMI = DefSrcReg->MI;
+    if (CondMI->getOpcode() != MOS::G_SBC)
+      return false;
+    if (MRI.getType(CondMI->getOperand(0).getReg()) != LLT::scalar(16))
+      return false;
+    auto CInConst =
+        getIConstantVRegValWithLookThrough(CondMI->getOperand(7).getReg(), MRI);
+    if (!CInConst || CInConst->Value.isZero())
+      return false;
+    LHS = CondMI->getOperand(5).getReg();
+    Flag = getSbcFlagForRegister(*CondMI, DefSrcReg->Reg);
+    return Flag == MOS::Z; // only equality (Z); native s16 ordering uses C, not NZ
+  }
+};
+
+struct CmpNZImm16_match : public CmpNZ16_match {
+  int64_t &RHS;
+  CmpNZImm16_match(Register &LHS, int64_t &RHS, Register &Flag)
+      : CmpNZ16_match(LHS, Flag), RHS(RHS) {}
+  bool match(const MachineRegisterInfo &MRI, Register CondReg) {
+    if (!CmpNZ16_match::match(MRI, CondReg))
+      return false;
+    // Recover the constant even when legalization byte-split it into G_MERGE_VALUES
+    // (a G_CONSTANT i16 is illegal under +mos-a16) — without this `g == 0x1234` and
+    // `*p == 0x1234` fall to CmpBrImag16 and materialize the constant into Imag16.
+    auto RHSConst = getI16Const(CondMI->getOperand(6).getReg(), MRI);
+    if (!RHSConst)
+      return false;
+    RHS = *RHSConst & 0xFFFF;
+    return true;
+  }
+};
+inline CmpNZImm16_match m_CmpNZImm16(Register &LHS, int64_t &RHS, Register &Flag) {
+  return {LHS, RHS, Flag};
+}
+
+struct CmpNZImag16_match : public CmpNZ16_match {
+  Register &RHS;
+  CmpNZImag16_match(Register &LHS, Register &RHS, Register &Flag)
+      : CmpNZ16_match(LHS, Flag), RHS(RHS) {}
+  bool match(const MachineRegisterInfo &MRI, Register CondReg) {
+    if (!CmpNZ16_match::match(MRI, CondReg))
+      return false;
+    RHS = CondMI->getOperand(6).getReg();
+    return true;
+  }
+};
+inline CmpNZImag16_match m_CmpNZImag16(Register &LHS, Register &RHS,
+                                       Register &Flag) {
+  return {LHS, RHS, Flag};
+}

+// Defined later (beside selectAlu16Native): extract a 16-bit constant operand.
+static std::optional<int64_t> getImm16Operand(Register R,
+                                              MachineRegisterInfo &MRI);
+
+// #321 native s16 compare-operand fold: returns the defining G_LOAD16_ABS iff `R`
+// is a single-use 16-bit near-abs global load in `User`'s block — the operand the
+// absolute compare/load forms (cmp abs / lda abs) can read directly instead of
+// #321 xy16: helpers to test whether a vreg is constrained to the 16-bit X or Y
+// register class. Used by selectXY16 and by foldableAbsLoad16's Xc16 guard.
+static bool isXc16Reg(Register R, const MachineRegisterInfo &MRI) {
+  if (R.isPhysical())
+    return MOS::Xc16RegClass.contains(R);
+  const TargetRegisterClass *RC = MRI.getRegClassOrNull(R);
+  return RC && RC->hasSuperClassEq(&MOS::Xc16RegClass);
+}
+static bool isYc16Reg(Register R, const MachineRegisterInfo &MRI) {
+  if (R.isPhysical())
+    return MOS::Yc16RegClass.contains(R);
+  const TargetRegisterClass *RC = MRI.getRegClassOrNull(R);
+  return RC && RC->hasSuperClassEq(&MOS::Yc16RegClass);
+}
+
+// round-tripping the global through an Imag16 pair. Volatile-safe: the load has
+// exactly this one use, so folding preserves the single memory access in program
+// order (single-use check, same as selectAlu16Native's abs operand fold). Same-BB
+// only (mirrors shouldFoldMemAccess's no-cross-block rule).
+// Guard: skip Xc16-constrained results — selectXY16 handles those via LDXAbs16
+// and must not be intercepted here (class conflict with the Ac16 LDAbs16 fold).
+
+// Folding a load into its user re-reads the memory at the USER's location, not the
+// load's. That is only value-preserving if nothing between the two CLOBBERS the loaded
+// memory. A call / unmodeled side effect could (e.g. `int tmp = g; clobber(); use(tmp,
+// g)`, gcc c-torture pr34768) -> bail. A store between only matters if it MAY ALIAS the
+// loaded address: AA-precision lets a provably non-aliasing store through (#321 Phase-1
+// Probe A — recovers folds the old conservative "any store" rule dropped). An *ordered*
+// (volatile/atomic) store is a hard bail regardless of aliasing, so a (possibly
+// volatile) load is never reordered across another volatile access. (Governing lesson
+// #2: a miss only forgoes a win, never regresses.) Def/User are single-use + same-BB,
+// so this scan is short and bounded; AA mirrors shouldFoldMemAccess's mayAlias check.
+static bool noClobberBetween(const MachineInstr *Def, const MachineInstr &User,
+                             AAResults *AA) {
+  bool afterDef = false;
+  for (const MachineInstr &MI : *Def->getParent()) {
+    if (&MI == Def) { afterDef = true; continue; }
+    if (&MI == &User)
+      break;
+    if (!afterDef)
+      continue;
+    if (MI.isCall() || MI.hasUnmodeledSideEffects())
+      return false;
+    if (MI.mayStore() &&
+        (MI.hasOrderedMemoryRef() || MI.mayAlias(AA, *Def, /*UseTBAA=*/true)))
+      return false;
+  }
+  return true;
+}
+
+static MachineInstr *foldableAbsLoad16(Register R, const MachineInstr &User,
+                                       const MachineRegisterInfo &MRI,
+                                       AAResults *AA) {
+  MachineInstr *Def = MRI.getVRegDef(R);
+  if (Def && Def->getOpcode() == MOS::G_LOAD16_ABS &&
+      MRI.hasOneNonDBGUse(R) && Def->getParent() == User.getParent() &&
+      !isXc16Reg(R, MRI) && noClobberBetween(Def, User, AA))
+    return Def;
+  return nullptr;
+}
+
+// #321 RHS-indexed compare fold: a single-use, same-BB 16-bit *indirect* load
+// (G_LOAD16_INDIR — the `lda (zp)` form that arr[i]/p[i] legalize to, via a
+// computed pointer) whose pointer `cmp (zp)` (CMPIndir16) can read directly, instead
+// of staging the loaded value through an Imag16 temp. Mirror of foldableAbsLoad16;
+// operand 1 is the zp pointer pair. No isXc16Reg guard: an indirect-load result is
+// never Xc16-constrained (only the abs G_LOAD16_ABS arm constrains for selectXY16).
+static MachineInstr *foldableIndirLoad16(Register R, const MachineInstr &User,
+                                         const MachineRegisterInfo &MRI,
+                                         AAResults *AA) {
+  MachineInstr *Def = MRI.getVRegDef(R);
+  if (Def && Def->getOpcode() == MOS::G_LOAD16_INDIR &&
+      MRI.hasOneNonDBGUse(R) && Def->getParent() == User.getParent() &&
+      noClobberBetween(Def, User, AA))
+    return Def;
+  return nullptr;
+}
+
+// #321 native 16-bit compares: select an s16 G_SBC that the legalizer left
+// un-narrowed for an unsigned-ordering compare (NativeS16/UGE path). Only the
+// carry-out flag (operand 1) is used; the s16 result and N/V/Z are dead and the
+// carry-in is set. Emits `lda lhs; cmp rhs` (both MLow=1 -> one rep/sep bracket)
+// producing C, which the following branch tests (bcc/bcs). A near-abs global
+// operand is read directly via lda abs / cmp abs (foldableAbsLoad16) instead of
+// being materialized into an Imag16 pair; otherwise the accumulator is
+// entered only via LDAImag16 -> never an Ac16<->8-bit COPY (the native invariant).
+bool MOSInstructionSelector::selectSbc16(MachineInstr &MI) {
+  MachineIRBuilder Builder(MI);
+  MachineRegisterInfo &MRI = *Builder.getMRI();
+
+  Register CarryOut = MI.getOperand(1).getReg(); // C — the only used flag (UGE)
+  Register L = MI.getOperand(5).getReg();        // LHS (s16, in Imag16 or abs load)
+  Register R = MI.getOperand(6).getReg();        // RHS (s16, in Imag16 or abs load)
+
+  SmallVector<MachineInstrBuilder, 2> New;
+  SmallVector<MachineInstr *, 2> Folded; // near-abs loads consumed by the fold
+
+  // lda lhs (16-bit) -> A16. A single-use near-abs global LHS reads via `lda abs`
+  // directly, dropping the `lda abs; sta tmp` round-trip; otherwise from its Imag16.
+  Register Lo = MRI.createVirtualRegister(&MOS::Ac16RegClass);
+  if (MachineInstr *LdL = foldableAbsLoad16(L, MI, MRI, AA)) {
+    New.push_back(Builder.buildInstr(MOS::LDAbs16)
+                      .addDef(Lo)
+                      .add(LdL->getOperand(1))
+                      .cloneMemRefs(*LdL));
+    Folded.push_back(LdL);
+  } else {
+    New.push_back(Builder.buildInstr(MOS::LDAImag16).addDef(Lo).addUse(L));
+  }
+
+  // cmp rhs (16-bit): A16 - rhs, discard result, set C/N/Z. A constant RHS folds to
+  // `cmp #imm16` (common loop bounds); a single-use near-abs global RHS to `cmp abs`
+  // (reading the global directly); otherwise compare the Imag16 operand.
+  if (auto Imm = getImm16Operand(R, MRI)) {
+    New.push_back(
+        Builder.buildInstr(MOS::CMPImm16).addDef(CarryOut).addUse(Lo).addImm(*Imm));
+  } else if (MachineInstr *LdR = foldableAbsLoad16(R, MI, MRI, AA)) {
+    New.push_back(Builder.buildInstr(MOS::CMPAbs16)
+                      .addDef(CarryOut)
+                      .addUse(Lo)
+                      .add(LdR->getOperand(1))
+                      .cloneMemRefs(*LdR));
+    Folded.push_back(LdR);
+  } else if (MachineInstr *LdR = foldableIndirLoad16(R, MI, MRI, AA)) {
+    // arr[i]/p[i] on the RHS: read the value directly via `cmp (zp)` (CMPIndir16)
+    // instead of staging it through an Imag16 temp. The pointer (operand 1) use is
+    // transferred to the cmp; the single-use indirect load is erased below.
+    New.push_back(Builder.buildInstr(MOS::CMPIndir16)
+                      .addDef(CarryOut)
+                      .addUse(Lo)
+                      .addUse(LdR->getOperand(1).getReg())
+                      .cloneMemRefs(*LdR));
+    Folded.push_back(LdR);
+  } else {
+    New.push_back(
+        Builder.buildInstr(MOS::CMPImag16).addDef(CarryOut).addUse(Lo).addUse(R));
+  }
+
+  for (MachineInstrBuilder &B : New)
+    constrainSelectedInstRegOperands(*B.getInstr(), TII, TRI, RBI);
+
+  // Erase the folded loads (single-use; this G_SBC was their only consumer, and
+  // selection is bottom-up so they are not yet selected).
+  for (MachineInstr *F : Folded)
+    F->eraseFromParent();
+
+  MI.eraseFromParent();
+  return true;
+}

+bool MOSInstructionSelector::selectReturnAddressByte(MachineInstr &MI) {
+  MachineIRBuilder Builder(MI);
+  MachineRegisterInfo &MRI = *Builder.getMRI();
+  Register Dst = MI.getOperand(0).getReg();
+  int64_t Byte = MI.getOperand(1).getImm();
+
+  MachineInstrBuilder Instr;
+  if (STI.hasW65816()) {
+    Instr = Builder.buildInstr(MOS::ReturnAddressByteSR, {Dst}, {}).addImm(Byte);
+  } else {
+    Register Scratch = MRI.createVirtualRegister(&MOS::XcRegClass);
+    Instr =
+        Builder.buildInstr(MOS::ReturnAddressByte, {Dst, Scratch}, {}).addImm(Byte);
+  }
+  constrainSelectedInstRegOperands(*Instr, TII, TRI, RBI);
+  MI.eraseFromParent();
+  return true;
+}

+// #320 far runtime: is this address-of operand a far (addrspace 2) symbol whose
+// address is a 24-bit value (Imag32/p2), not a 16-bit near pointer? Two cases:
+//   * a far-addrspace (AS_Far) global — far DATA, declared address_space(2).
+//   * a FUNCTION placed in a .far_* section — how a far-banked routine is marked
+//     (far functions are AS0 + .far_text, so the AS check above misses them).
+// The .far* section check is restricted to FUNCTIONS on purpose: far DATA must
+// opt in via address_space(2). A plain (AS0) data symbol merely PLACED in a
+// .far_* section (e.g. a `.far_rodata` constant) keeps a near 16-bit address —
+// taking &it as a near pointer (e.g. to build a 24-bit address by hand) must not
+// over-trigger 24-bit materialization into a 16-bit context (which crashed
+// selectAddrLoHi/buildFarAddrWords; gate dev/run.sh far_indir).
+static bool isFarSymbol(const MachineOperand &Op) {
+  if (!Op.isGlobal())
+    return false;
+  const GlobalValue *GV = Op.getGlobal();
+  if (GV->getAddressSpace() == MOS::AS_Far)
+    return true;
+  if (const auto *GO = dyn_cast<GlobalObject>(GV->getAliaseeObject())) {
+    if (GO->getAddressSpace() == MOS::AS_Far)
+      return true;
+    if (isa<Function>(GO) && GO->hasSection() &&
+        GO->getSection().starts_with(".far"))
+      return true;
+  }
+  return false;
+}
+
+std::pair<Register, Register>
+MOSInstructionSelector::buildFarAddrWords(MachineIRBuilder &Builder,
+                                          const MachineOperand &GO) {
+  LLT S8 = LLT::scalar(8);
+  LLT S16 = LLT::scalar(16);
+  MachineRegisterInfo &MRI = *Builder.getMRI();
+
+  const auto &BuildByte = [&](unsigned Flag) -> Register {
+    auto I = buildLdImm(Builder, S8).add(GO);
+    I->getOperand(1).setTargetFlags(Flag);
+    constrainSelectedInstRegOperands(*I, TII, TRI, RBI);
+    return I.getReg(0);
+  };
+
+  // Low word = segment (address bits 0-15).
+  Register SegLo = BuildByte(MOS::MO_ADDR24_SEG_LO);
+  Register SegHi = BuildByte(MOS::MO_ADDR24_SEG_HI);
+  Register SegW = MRI.createGenericVirtualRegister(S16);
+  composePtr(Builder, SegW, SegLo, SegHi);
+
+  // High word = (bank, 0): the program bank (bits 16-23) in the low byte, the
+  // p2's unused top byte zero.
+  Register Bank = BuildByte(MOS::MO_ADDR24_BANK);
+  auto Zero = buildLdImm(Builder, S8).addImm(0);
+  constrainSelectedInstRegOperands(*Zero, TII, TRI, RBI);
+  Register BankW = MRI.createGenericVirtualRegister(S16);
+  composePtr(Builder, BankW, Bank, Zero.getReg(0));
+
+  return {SegW, BankW};
+}
+
 bool MOSInstructionSelector::selectAddr(MachineInstr &MI) {
   MachineIRBuilder Builder(MI);
   MachineOperand Op = MI.getOperand(1);
   if (Op.isCImm())
     Op.ChangeToImmediate(Op.getCImm()->getSExtValue());
 
+  // #320 far runtime: the address of a far symbol is a 24-bit value held in an
+  // Imag32 (p2). Compose it from the segment word + (bank, 0) word.
+  if (isFarSymbol(Op)) {
+    auto [SegW, BankW] = buildFarAddrWords(Builder, Op);
+    auto RegSeq = Builder.buildInstr(MOS::REG_SEQUENCE)
+                      .addDef(MI.getOperand(0).getReg())
+                      .addUse(SegW)
+                      .addImm(MOS::sublo16)
+                      .addUse(BankW)
+                      .addImm(MOS::subhi16);
+    constrainGenericOp(*RegSeq);
+    MI.eraseFromParent();
+    return true;
+  }
+
   MachineInstrBuilder Instr = buildLdImm(Builder, MI.getOperand(0))
                                   .add(Op);
   constrainSelectedInstRegOperands(*Instr, TII, TRI, RBI);
